Run: `pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts`
Expected: FAIL -- `../../src/primitives/segmented-reduce.js` cannot be resolved (the pure describe of Step 1 still passes once the module exists; until then the whole file fails to load).

- [ ] **Step 10: The driver -- implementation**

Create `src/primitives/segmented-reduce.ts` (the declarations of contract 3.11 verbatim; PLAN DECISIONS: the row count and arc count are derived from the binding sizes of the core -- the residency binds every array at its exact byte length, so `rowPtr.size = 4(n + 1)` and `colIdx.size = 4 x arcCount` -- because `record()` receives no snapshot; a windowed core is `E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "segmentedReduce.windowed" }`; the snippet vocabulary check follows contract 4.5 / 3.11 literally -- "one or more WGSL statements assigning v" whose IDENTIFIERS are only `row`, `arc`, `nbr`, `weight`, `v` -- so the WGSL keywords a statement needs (`if else let var const true false`), the builtin scalar types and a bounded list of builtin math functions are not identifiers and pass, while a binding or uniform name (`rowPtr colIdx weights perm out P`), a reserved word (`target`), a locally declared name, a flow keyword that would leave the fold (`return break continue`) and any other word are `E_SHADER_COMPILE { stage: "compose", slot: "VALUE", identifier }`; P7 widens the vocabulary per kernel when the pull kernel needs `xNorm[nbr]`):

```ts
/**
 * The segmented (per-row) reduction primitive of spec 6 row 3 in its thread-per-row tier (P2-P3): one invocation per
 * CSR row folds the caller's VALUE snippet over the row's arcs into `out[row]` (f32), a row with no arcs receiving
 * the identity element. The degree tiers of degreeOrder() (subgroup-per-row for the mid tier, workgroup-per-row for
 * the high tier) land at P4; until then `tiers !== null` is E_UNSUPPORTED and USE_PERM is always false (the perm
 * slot carries the rowPtr dummy of graphBindings). The row and arc counts come from the core's binding sizes: the
 * residency binds every array at its exact byte length (contract 3.8), so rowPtr is 4(n + 1) bytes and colIdx
 * 4 x arcCount.
 */

import { WebGpuGraphError } from "../errors.js";
import { plan1d } from "../kernel/dispatch.js";
import { type Kernel } from "../kernel/kernel.js";
import { graphBindings, graphOverrides, kernelSpec, RANGE_PARAMS } from "../kernels.js";
import { type CoreBinding } from "../memory/residency.js";
import { type Binding } from "../types/memory.js";
import { type ReduceOp, type ReduceScope } from "./reduce.js";

/** The degree tiers of degreeOrder(): the permutation binding and the CPU-side segmentOffsets [0, hiEnd, midEnd, lowEnd, n]. */
export interface DegreeTiers {
    readonly perm: Binding;
    readonly segmentOffsets: readonly [number, number, number, number, number];
}

/** Options of segmentedReduce. `valueSnippet` is the Gunrock-style functor: WGSL statements assigning `v` from (row, arc, nbr, weight) (4.5; `nbr` because `target` is a WGSL reserved word). */
export interface SegmentedReduceOptions {
    readonly op: ReduceOp;
    readonly valueSnippet: string;
    readonly tiers: DegreeTiers | null;
    readonly accumulate?: boolean | undefined;
}

/** A prepared segmented reduce (P2-P3: the thread-per-row tier only; `tiers !== null` -> E_UNSUPPORTED { feature: "segmentedReduce.tiers" } until P4). */
export interface SegmentedReducePlanner {
    /** Records one dispatch over rows [0, n) (tiers null) writing out[i] (f32) per row; a row with no arcs gets the identity element. */
    record(pass: GPUComputePassEncoder, core: CoreBinding, out: Binding): void;
}

/** The identifiers a VALUE snippet may name (contract 3.11, 4.5); every other identifier is rejected textually before compose. */
const VALUE_SNIPPET_VOCABULARY: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(["row", "arc", "nbr", "weight", "v"]);

/**
 * The WGSL words a snippet statement may use that are not identifiers: the statement keywords (never the flow
 * keywords `return` / `break` / `continue`, which would leave the fold), the scalar type constructors and the
 * builtin math functions a value expression may call (a bounded list; a binding, a uniform or a module function such
 * as `identity` / `comb` / `linear_id` is NOT in it and is rejected as an identifier).
 */
const VALUE_SNIPPET_WGSL_WORDS: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set([
    "if",
    "else",
    "let",
    "var",
    "const",
    "true",
    "false",
    "f32",
    "u32",
    "i32",
    "bool",
    "abs",
    "ceil",
    "clamp",
    "exp",
    "exp2",
    "floor",
    "fract",
    "inverseSqrt",
    "log",
    "log2",
    "max",
    "min",
    "mix",
    "pow",
    "round",
    "select",
    "sign",
    "sqrt",
    "step",
    "trunc",
]);

/**
 * The OP override value of an operator (the body's `OP == 1u` / `OP == 2u` tests, contract 4.5).
 * @param op - the operator
 * @returns 0 for sum, 1 for min, 2 for max
 */
function opCode(op: ReduceOp): number {
    switch (op) {
        case "sum":
            return 0;
        case "min":
            return 1;
        case "max":
            return 2;
        default:
            throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_INVALID_ARGUMENT", `segmentedReduce: unknown op ${String(op)}`, {
                argument: "op",
                value: op,
                expected: "sum | min | max",
            });
    }
}

/**
 * WGSL comments removed (a comment may mention `target` or any other word).
 * @param text - WGSL text
 * @returns the text with block and line comments replaced by spaces
 */
function stripComments(text: string): string {
    return text.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, " ").replace(/\/\/[^\n]*/g, " ");
}

/**
 * The textual vocabulary check of contract 3.11: the snippet must assign `v` (`=`, `+=`, `-=`, `*=` or `/=`) and its
 * identifiers may be only row, arc, nbr, weight and v; numeric literals and the WGSL words of
 * VALUE_SNIPPET_WGSL_WORDS are not identifiers and pass; anything else -- a binding, a uniform, a module function,
 * a locally declared name, a flow keyword, a reserved word such as `target` -- is E_SHADER_COMPILE { stage:
 * "compose", slot: "VALUE", identifier } on every device, before any shader is created.
 * @param snippet - the VALUE snippet text
 */
function validateValueSnippet(snippet: string): void {
    const code = stripComments(snippet);
    if (!/\bv\s*[-+*/]?=(?!=)/.test(code)) {
        throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_SHADER_COMPILE", "segmentedReduce: the VALUE snippet never assigns v", {
            id: "segmented-reduce",
            stage: "compose",
            slot: "VALUE",
        });
    }
    const tokens = code.match(/[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*|[0-9][0-9A-Za-z_.]*/g) ?? [];
    for (const token of tokens) {
        if (/^[0-9]/.test(token) || VALUE_SNIPPET_VOCABULARY.has(token) || VALUE_SNIPPET_WGSL_WORDS.has(token)) {
            continue;
        }
        throw new WebGpuGraphError(
            "E_SHADER_COMPILE",
            `segmentedReduce: the VALUE snippet names "${token}"; the only identifiers allowed are row, arc, nbr, weight and v`,
            { id: "segmented-reduce", stage: "compose", slot: "VALUE", identifier: token },
        );
    }
}

/**
 * The row count of a core from its rowPtr binding (4(n + 1) bytes).
 * @param core - the core
 * @returns n
 */
function rowCountOf(core: CoreBinding): number {
    const bytes = core.rowPtr.size;
    if (bytes < 4 || bytes % 4 !== 0) {
        throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_INVALID_ARGUMENT", `segmentedReduce: a rowPtr binding of ${bytes} bytes is not 4(n + 1)`, {
            argument: "core.rowPtr",
            value: bytes,
            expected: "a positive multiple of 4",
        });
    }
    return bytes / 4 - 1;
}

/**
 * Rejects a windowed core (executed at P4).
 * @param core - the core
 */
function assertNotWindowed(core: CoreBinding): void {
    if (core.plan === "windowed" || core.windows !== null) {
        throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_UNSUPPORTED", "segmentedReduce: windowed cores are executed at P4", {
            feature: "segmentedReduce.windowed",
        });
    }
}

/** The thread-per-row planner: ONE `segmented-reduce` dispatch with TIER 0 over every row. */
class ThreadPerRowPlanner implements SegmentedReducePlanner {
    private readonly scope: ReduceScope;
    private readonly kernel: Kernel;
    private readonly hasWeights: boolean;
    private readonly accumulate: boolean;

    /**
     * Wraps a compiled thread-per-row pipeline with the pattern it was compiled for.
     * @param scope - the scope the pipeline was prepared in
     * @param kernel - the compiled kernel
     * @param hasWeights - the HAS_WEIGHTS the pipeline was compiled with
     * @param accumulate - whether record() combines into out instead of overwriting
     */
    constructor(scope: ReduceScope, kernel: Kernel, hasWeights: boolean, accumulate: boolean) {
        this.scope = scope;
        this.kernel = kernel;
        this.hasWeights = hasWeights;
        this.accumulate = accumulate;
    }

    /**
     * Records the dispatch: rows [0, n), arcs [0, arcCount), plan1d(n); nothing for n = 0 (no zero-length binding is
     * ever created).
     * @param pass - the pass to record into
     * @param core - a core with the SAME weights pattern as the one prepared (any snapshot)
     * @param out - at least 4n bytes of f32
     */
    record(pass: GPUComputePassEncoder, core: CoreBinding, out: Binding): void {
        assertNotWindowed(core);
        if ((core.weights !== null) !== this.hasWeights) {
            throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_INVALID_ARGUMENT", "segmentedReduce: the core's weights pattern differs from the one prepared", {
                argument: "core",
                value: core.weights !== null,
                expected: this.hasWeights,
            });
        }
        const n = rowCountOf(core);
        if (n === 0) {
            return;
        }
        const arcCount = core.colIdx === null ? 0 : core.colIdx.size / 4;
        if (out.size < 4 * n) {
            throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_INVALID_ARGUMENT", `segmentedReduce: out holds ${out.size} bytes, ${4 * n} needed`, {
                argument: "out",
                value: out.size,
                expected: `>= ${4 * n}`,
            });
        }
        const params = this.scope.params(RANGE_PARAMS, {
            start: 0,
            end: n,
            arcBase: 0,
            arcEnd: arcCount,
            accumulate: this.accumulate ? 1 : 0,
            n,
        });
        const bound = this.kernel.bind({ ...graphBindings(core, null), out, P: params.binding });
        this.kernel.dispatch(pass, bound, plan1d(n, this.scope.workgroupSize, this.scope.caps), [params.offset]);
    }
}

/**
 * Prepares the thread-per-row pipeline for a snapshot's dummy pattern (USE_PERM, HAS_WEIGHTS) and snippet.
 * @param scope - the reduce scope (pipelines, pool, params writer)
 * @param core - the core whose weights pattern selects HAS_WEIGHTS (USE_PERM is false: no tiers at P2)
 * @param options - operator, snippet, tiers (must be null), accumulate
 * @returns the planner
 */
export async function prepareSegmentedReduce(
    scope: ReduceScope,
    core: CoreBinding,
    options: SegmentedReduceOptions,
): Promise<SegmentedReducePlanner> {
    if (options.tiers !== null) {
        throw new WebGpuGraphError("E_UNSUPPORTED", "segmentedReduce: the degree tiers land at P4; pass tiers: null", {
            feature: "segmentedReduce.tiers",
        });
    }
    assertNotWindowed(core);
    const op = opCode(options.op);
    validateValueSnippet(options.valueSnippet);
    const overrides = { ...graphOverrides(core, null), OP: op, TIER: 0 };
    const spec = kernelSpec("segmented-reduce", overrides, { VALUE: options.valueSnippet });
    const kernel = await scope.pipelines.kernel(spec);
    return new ThreadPerRowPlanner(scope, kernel, core.weights !== null, options.accumulate === true);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 12: The sabotage rows -- the table edit**

Edit `test/helpers/sabotage.ts` (P1-T5's file; this task owns the segmented-reduce rows): add the key to the `SABOTAGE` table and "P2" to `SABOTAGE_PHASES`. PLAN DECISION on the row-bound mutation (a departure from the normative example of contract 5.5, pending owner confirmation at G2 alongside the four contract corrections of the review log): the contract's `row >= P.end` -> `row > P.end` form adds one out-of-range invocation (`row == n`) whose store `out[n]` lands INSIDE the out buffer whenever the pool's size class rounds the buffer above 4n bytes, and otherwise in a clamped slot that races with row n - 1's own store -- so whether the mutant is caught depends on the pool and the driver's lane order, and the contract's form is not tested at all; `row + 1u >= P.end` skips the last row deterministically (its sentinel survives) and is the form used, which is also spec 11.9 item 1's own wording ("`n - 1` instead of `n` in the bound"). The `test` field follows the path convention of the P1 rows (package-relative; if P1-T5 stored basenames, use `segmented-reduce.test.ts`).

```ts

        {
            // contract 5.5 spells this row `row >= P.end` -> `row > P.end`; that mutant's extra invocation writes
            // out[n], an in-bounds slot under the pool's size classes, so it is not reliably caught. The form here
            // skips the last row deterministically (spec 11.9 item 1: "n - 1 instead of n in the bound"). Pending
            // owner confirmation, docs/decisions/G2.md.
            name: "last-row-skipped",
            find: "row >= P.end",
            replace: "row + 1u >= P.end",
            minFactor: 10,
            test: "test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts",
        },
        {
            name: "weight-read-dropped",
            find: "weight = weights[arc - P.arcBase]",
            replace: "weight = 1.0",
            minFactor: 10,
            test: "test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts",
        },
        {
            name: "has-weights-inverted",
            find: "if (HAS_WEIGHTS)",
            replace: "if (!HAS_WEIGHTS)",
            minFactor: 10,
            test: "test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts",
        },
        {
            name: "min-identity-zero",
            find: "return F32_MAX;",
            replace: "return 0.0;",
            minFactor: 10,
            test: "test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts",
        },
        {
            name: "empty-rows-skipped",

            replace: "if (a1 > a0) { out[i] = select(acc, comb(out[i], acc), P.accumulate == 1u); }",

```

and

```ts
export const SABOTAGE_PHASES: readonly ("P1" | "P2" | "P3")[] = ["P1", "P2"];
```

Why each mutant fails the check set: `last-row-skipped` leaves row 999 of random1k at the sentinel; `weight-read-dropped` and `has-weights-inverted` sum 1.0 per arc against weights averaging 2.1 (a ~50% error; the inverted form reads the colIdx dummy as f32 on unweighted cores, which the set does not need); `min-identity-zero` makes every row's min 0 against values >= 0.25; `empty-rows-skipped` leaves rows 200..299 of the 300-row check at the sentinel; `perm-select-swapped` reads `perm[row]` (the rowPtr dummy) as the row index, so every row outside the image of rowPtr keeps the sentinel. Every `find` occurs exactly once in the body (`return -F32_MAX;` does not contain `return F32_MAX;`).

- [ ] **Step 13: The determinism test**

Create `test/kernel/determinism.test.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * Spec 11.9 item 4 for the two reduction primitives (contract 5.5 P2): reduce (f32 sum) and the thread-per-row
 * segmented-reduce give bitwise identical results on two runs, on the subgroup twin and on the workgroup twin; and
 * the subgroup helper's slot-ranking mechanism (contract 4.3, D16) is verified on the device by an ad hoc kernel
 * that records which subgroup key took which slot and the rank order the helper folds in.
 */

import { type F32, type U32 } from "@graphty/graph-format";
import { type TestContext } from "vitest";

import { type GpuContext } from "../../src/context.js";
import { plan1d } from "../../src/kernel/dispatch.js";
import { type WgslModuleSpec } from "../../src/kernel/wgsl.js";
import { prepareReduce } from "../../src/primitives/reduce.js";
import { bindingOf, readF32, readU32, withContext } from "../helpers/device.js";
import { expectBitwiseEqual } from "../helpers/matchers.js";
import { SEGMENTED_REDUCE_SNIPPETS } from "../helpers/override-matrix.js";
import { runSegmentedReduce, testReduceScope, weightedRandom } from "../helpers/segmented-reduce.js";
import { acquire, requireGpu } from "../setup/gpu.js";

/** Seeded f32 values in [-1, 1) (mixed signs, so the summation order matters). */
function makeValues(count: number, seed: number): F32 {
    const values = new Float32Array(count);
    let state = seed % 4294967296;
    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
        state = (state * 1664525 + 1013904223) % 4294967296;
        values[i] = 2 * (state / 4294967296) - 1;
    }
    return values;
}

/** Two reduce sums of the same values on one context. */
async function reduceSumTwice(ctx: GpuContext, values: F32): Promise<[F32, F32]> {
    const scope = testReduceScope(ctx);
    try {
        const src = scope.scratch(values.byteLength, "determinism/src");
        ctx.device.queue.writeBuffer(src, 0, values);
        const out = scope.scratch(16, "determinism/out");
        const planner = await prepareReduce(scope, "sum", "f32");
        const results: F32[] = [];
        for (let run = 0; run < 2; run++) {
            const encoder = ctx.device.createCommandEncoder({ label: "determinism/reduce" });
            const pass = encoder.beginComputePass();
            planner.record(pass, bindingOf(src), values.length, bindingOf(out), 0);
            pass.end();
            ctx.device.queue.submit([encoder.finish()]);
            results.push(await readF32(ctx, out, 1));
        }
        expect(planner.lastDispatches).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
        return [results[0], results[1]];
    } finally {
        scope.dispose();
    }
}

/** The ad hoc kernel: every subgroup's elected lane takes a slot from an atomic counter (the D16 mechanism) and records its key (the subgroup's smallest local id); lanes below the count then rank the keys exactly as the helper does. */
const SLOT_ORDER_BODY = /* wgsl */ `
var<workgroup> so_counter: atomic<u32>;
var<workgroup> so_keys: array<u32, 64>;

@compute @workgroup_size(WG)
fn slot_order(@builtin(local_invocation_id) lid: vec3<u32>) {
    if (lid.x == 0u) { atomicStore(&so_counter, 0u); }
    workgroupBarrier();
    let key = subgroupMin(lid.x);
    var slot = 0u;
    if (subgroupElect()) { slot = atomicAdd(&so_counter, 1u); }
    slot = subgroupBroadcast(slot, 0u);
    if (subgroupElect()) { so_keys[slot] = key; }
    workgroupBarrier();
    let count = atomicLoad(&so_counter);
    if (lid.x < count) {
        let mine = so_keys[lid.x];
        var rank = 0u;
        for (var k = 0u; k < count; k = k + 1u) { if (so_keys[k] < mine) { rank = rank + 1u; } }
        slotKey[lid.x] = mine;
        ranked[rank] = mine;
    }
    let total = wg_reduce_u32(1u, lid.x, 0u);
    if (lid.x == 0u) { meta[0] = count; meta[1] = total; meta[2] = WG; }
}
`;

const SLOT_ORDER_SPEC: WgslModuleSpec = {
    id: "sg-slot-order",
    body: SLOT_ORDER_BODY,
    bindings: [
        { group: 1, binding: 0, name: "slotKey", kind: "storage", wgslType: "array<u32>" },
        { group: 1, binding: 1, name: "ranked", kind: "storage", wgslType: "array<u32>" },
        { group: 1, binding: 2, name: "meta", kind: "storage", wgslType: "array<u32>" },
    ],
    overrideDecls: [],
    overrides: {},
    needs: ["subgroups"],
    uniforms: [],
};

describe("determinism (spec 11.9 item 4)", () => {
    let shared: GpuContext | null = null;

    async function context(t: TestContext): Promise<GpuContext> {
        requireGpu(t);
        const ctx = shared ?? (await acquire({ label: "determinism" }));
        shared = ctx;
        return ctx;
    }

    it("reduce f32 sum over 65,537 values: two runs bitwise on the subgroup twin and on the workgroup twin", async (t) => {
        const ctx = await context(t);
        const values = makeValues(65_537, 11);
        const [a, b] = await reduceSumTwice(ctx, values);
        expectBitwiseEqual(a, b, "reduce twice (feature context)");
        const [c, d] = await withContext({ subgroups: false, label: "determinism-twin" }, (twin) => reduceSumTwice(twin, values));
        expectBitwiseEqual(c, d, "reduce twice (workgroup twin)");
    });

    it("segmented-reduce: two runs bitwise on both twins", async (t) => {
        const ctx = await context(t);
        const s = weightedRandom(2000, 8000, 5);
        const snippet = SEGMENTED_REDUCE_SNIPPETS.weight;
        const a = await runSegmentedReduce(ctx, s, "sum", snippet);
        const b = await runSegmentedReduce(ctx, s, "sum", snippet);
        expectBitwiseEqual(a, b, "segmented-reduce twice (feature context)");
        const [c, d] = await withContext({ subgroups: false, label: "determinism-twin" }, async (twin) => {
            const first = await runSegmentedReduce(twin, s, "sum", snippet);
            const second = await runSegmentedReduce(twin, s, "sum", snippet);
            twin.release(s);
            return [first, second];
        });
        expectBitwiseEqual(c, d, "segmented-reduce twice (workgroup twin)");
        expectBitwiseEqual(a, c, "segmented-reduce across twins");
        ctx.release(s);
    });

    it("the subgroup helper ranks its slots by key: the fold order is fixed whatever order the elected lanes took their slots", async (t) => {
        const ctx = await context(t);
        if (!ctx.caps.features.has("subgroups")) {
            t.skip("the device has no subgroups feature: no slots to rank (GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS run)");
        }
        const kernel = await ctx.pipelines.kernel(SLOT_ORDER_SPEC);
        const wg = ctx.workgroupSize;
        const scope = testReduceScope(ctx);
        try {
            const rankedRuns: U32[] = [];
            for (let run = 0; run < 2; run++) {
                const slotKey = scope.scratch(64 * 4, "slot-order/slotKey");
                const ranked = scope.scratch(64 * 4, "slot-order/ranked");
                const meta = scope.scratch(16, "slot-order/meta");
                const bound = kernel.bind({ slotKey: bindingOf(slotKey), ranked: bindingOf(ranked), meta: bindingOf(meta) });
                const encoder = ctx.device.createCommandEncoder({ label: "slot-order" });
                const pass = encoder.beginComputePass();
                kernel.dispatch(pass, bound, plan1d(wg, wg, ctx.caps));
                pass.end();
                ctx.device.queue.submit([encoder.finish()]);
                const m = await readU32(ctx, meta, 4);
                const count = m[0];
                expect(m[1]).toBe(wg); // wg_reduce_u32 of 1 over the workgroup
                expect(m[2]).toBe(wg);
                expect(count).toBeGreaterThan(0);
                const subgroupSize = wg / count;
                expect(count * subgroupSize).toBe(wg);
                expect(subgroupSize).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(4);
                if (ctx.caps.subgroupMinSize > 0) {
                    expect(subgroupSize).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(ctx.caps.subgroupMinSize);
                }
                if (ctx.caps.subgroupMaxSize > 0) {
                    expect(subgroupSize).toBeLessThanOrEqual(ctx.caps.subgroupMaxSize);
                }
                const keys = await readU32(ctx, slotKey, count);
                const rankedKeys = await readU32(ctx, ranked, count);
                for (let r = 0; r < count; r++) {
                    expect(rankedKeys[r]).toBe(r * subgroupSize); // the ranked order is 0, s, 2s, ...: fixed
                }
                expect([...keys].sort((x, y) => x - y)).toEqual([...rankedKeys]); // the slots hold the same keys in whatever order
                rankedRuns.push(rankedKeys);
            }
            expectBitwiseEqual(rankedRuns[0], rankedRuns[1], "ranked order across two runs");
        } finally {
            scope.dispose();
        }
    });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 14: The compile matrix in the browser and the browser noise leg**

Create `test/browser/compile-matrix.test.ts`:

```ts
/**
 * The browser twin of test/kernel/wgsl-compile.test.ts (spec 5.1, 11.3, 11.6 item 6; contract 5.5 P2): every
 * OVERRIDE_MATRIX case compiles on Chromium (SwiftShader on the default lane, NVIDIA on the GPU lane), twin cases
 * also on a second context created without the subgroups feature (the workgroup twin's browser leg, spec 11.6 item
 * 5), which catches uniform-layout bugs Dawn-node's uniform_buffer_standard_layout masks. PLAN DECISION (P2-T2): the
 * file also runs the thread-per-row segmented-reduce on the weighted random1k graph so the browser adapters' noise
 * fixtures and oracle rows exist (G2: "noise-floor rows recorded from the three adapters"); the cross-adapter
 * comparison itself runs in the node primitive test against every committed fixture.
 */

import { type TestContext } from "vitest";

import { type GpuContext } from "../../src/context.js";
import { type KernelId, KERNELS, kernelSpec } from "../../src/kernels.js";
import { expectAllClose, expectBitwiseEqual, maxRelError } from "../helpers/matchers.js";
import { adapterClassBrowser, recordNoiseRowBrowser, writeNoiseFixtureBrowser } from "../helpers/noise-floor-browser.js";
import { entryOf, OVERRIDE_MATRIX, type OverrideCase, SEGMENTED_REDUCE_SNIPPETS } from "../helpers/override-matrix.js";
import { maxAbsError, oracleValueOf, relTolerance, runSegmentedReduce, SR_ABS_FLOOR, weightedRandom } from "../helpers/segmented-reduce.js";
import { segmentedReduceOracle } from "../oracle/segmented-reduce.js";
import { acquireBrowser, requireBrowserGpu } from "../setup/browser.js";

describe("compile matrix on Chromium", () => {
    let ctx: GpuContext | null = null;
    let twin: GpuContext | null = null;

    async function contexts(t: TestContext): Promise<{ ctx: GpuContext; twin: GpuContext }> {
        await requireBrowserGpu(t);
        const a = ctx ?? (await acquireBrowser({ label: "compile-matrix" }));
        ctx = a;
        const b = twin ?? (await acquireBrowser({ optionalFeatures: [], label: "compile-matrix-twin" }));
        twin = b;
        return { ctx: a, twin: b };
    }

    async function compileAll(context: GpuContext, cases: readonly OverrideCase[]): Promise<void> {
        for (const c of cases) {
            const kernel = await context.pipelines.kernel(kernelSpec(c.id, c.overrides, c.snippets));
            expect(kernel.entryPoint).toBe(entryOf(c.id).entryPoint);
        }
    }

    it("the feature context has subgroups (both CI browser adapters expose it) and the twin does not", async (t) => {
        const { ctx: a, twin: b } = await contexts(t);
        expect(a.caps.features.has("subgroups")).toBe(true);
        expect(b.caps.features.has("subgroups")).toBe(false);
    });

    for (const id of Object.keys(KERNELS) as KernelId[]) {
        it(`compiles every case of ${id} on Chromium (twin cases on both feature settings)`, async (t) => {
            const { ctx: a, twin: b } = await contexts(t);
            const cases = OVERRIDE_MATRIX.filter((c) => c.id === id);
            expect(cases.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
            await compileAll(a, cases);
            const twins = cases.filter((c) => c.twin);
            if (twins.length > 0) {
                await compileAll(b, twins);
            }
        });
    }

    it("compiled the whole matrix once (bounded: pipelines.size equals the case count)", async (t) => {
        const { ctx: a } = await contexts(t);
        expect(a.pipelines.size).toBe(OVERRIDE_MATRIX.length);
    });

    it("segmented-reduce thread-per-row on random1k (weighted sum): oracle within the analytic bound, twice bitwise, this adapter's noise fixture and oracle row written", async (t) => {
        const { ctx: a } = await contexts(t);
        const s = weightedRandom(1000, 5000, 7);
        const snippet = SEGMENTED_REDUCE_SNIPPETS.weight;
        const first = await runSegmentedReduce(a, s, "sum", snippet);
        const second = await runSegmentedReduce(a, s, "sum", snippet);
        expectBitwiseEqual(first, second, "twice");
        const expected = segmentedReduceOracle(s, oracleValueOf(snippet), "sum");
        expectAllClose(first, expected, { rel: relTolerance(s, "sum"), abs: 0 }, "random1k weighted sum");
        const mine = adapterClassBrowser(a.caps);
        const oracleErr = maxRelError(first, expected, SR_ABS_FLOOR);
        console.warn(`[segmented-reduce] ${mine}: oracle-f64 maxRelError ${oracleErr.toExponential(3)}`);
        const written = await writeNoiseFixtureBrowser("segmented-reduce", "random1k", mine, Array.from(first), "f32");
        expect(typeof written).toBe("string");
        await recordNoiseRowBrowser({
            id: `segmented-reduce.sum.oracle-f64.${mine}`,
            kernel: "segmented-reduce",
            fixture: "random1k",
            comparison: "oracle-f64",
            a: mine,
            b: "oracle-f64",
            maxRelError: oracleErr,
            maxAbsError: maxAbsError(first, expected),
            samples: s.nodeCount,
        });
        a.release(s);
    });
});
```

Every module this file pulls into the Chromium bundle is browser-safe: `test/helpers/segmented-reduce.ts` follows the import rule in its header (no `test/helpers/device.ts`, no `test/setup/gpu.ts`, no `node:*`), `test/helpers/noise-floor-browser.ts` is P1-T7's bridge client, and `graphs.ts` / `matchers.ts` / `override-matrix.ts` / `test/oracle/segmented-reduce.ts` import only `src/**` outside `src/node/**`. 1. NVIDIA node: `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1 GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts`
   Expected: PASS; creates `test/fixtures/noise/segmented-reduce-random1k-nvidia-lovelace-node.json` (shape `{ "kernel": "segmented-reduce", "fixture": "random1k", "adapterClass": "nvidia-lovelace-node", "dtype": "f32", "values": [ ...1000 numbers ] }`) and the row `segmented-reduce.sum.oracle-f64.nvidia-lovelace-node` in `benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json`; prints `oracle-f64 maxRelError` (expected order 1e-7: a few f32 roundings per row). Confirm the class with `grep -o '"id": "segmented-reduce.sum.oracle-f64.[^"]*"' benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json`.
2. Add the tolerance entry to `benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json` under `"tolerances"` by the rule `test/noise-floor.test.ts` (P1-T6) validates -- it is the arbiter, so its arithmetic is repeated here exactly: `basis` = the id of the NVIDIA oracle row of item 1, copied from the file; `effective` = max(the basis row's `maxRelError`, 2^-24 = 5.9604644775390625e-8) -- the test's `MIN_FLOOR`, one f32 ulp, which is the floor whenever the measured error is below it; `value` = 10 x effective rounded DOWN to one significant figure (so effective <= value <= 10 x effective holds on both sides; with effective = 2^-24 that is 5e-7); `factor` = value / effective written as the full double `JSON.stringify` prints (the test allows 1e-6 relative slack, so a hand-rounded `8.4` fails). The shape:
   ```json
   "segmented-reduce.cross": { "value": 5e-7, "basis": "segmented-reduce.sum.oracle-f64.nvidia-lovelace-node", "factor": 8.388608 }
   ```
   (those numbers are the 2^-24 case: 5e-7 / 5.9604644775390625e-8 = 8.388608; write the measured ones, and the class the file holds). Then run `pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/noise-floor.test.ts` -- expected PASS -- BEFORE the lavapipe run of item 3, so a wrong entry is caught before the cross rows exist. PLAN DECISION: the cross-adapter tolerance takes an `oracle-f64` row as its basis because the cross-adapter row itself is expected to be exactly 0 -- the thread-per-row fold adds in index order with no FMA, so every adapter produces the same f32 bits -- and a zero basis leaves the factor undefined.
3. lavapipe node: `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1 GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER=llvmpipe GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any VK_DRIVER_FILES=/usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.x86_64.json XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts`

4. SwiftShader browser: `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE=1 GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=swiftshader GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp pnpm exec vitest run --project=browser test/browser/compile-matrix.test.ts`
   Expected: PASS; creates `segmented-reduce-random1k-google-swiftshader-browser.json` and the row `segmented-reduce.sum.oracle-f64.google-swiftshader-browser` through the commands bridge.

6. If any adapter's oracle-f64 `maxRelError` exceeds the NVIDIA one, re-point the tolerance's `basis` to that row and recompute `effective` / `value` / `factor` by the rule of item 2, then re-run `test/noise-floor.test.ts`.
7. Confirm with the non-writing runs: the NVIDIA and lavapipe commands of Step 10 (without `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE`) -- expected PASS, each printing one cross-adapter line per committed foreign fixture, all `<= value`; and `pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/noise-floor.test.ts` -- expected PASS (the tolerance is >= its effective floor and <= 10x it, its factor consistent).

  Object.freeze(["--enable-unsafe-webgpu", "--enable-features=Vulkan", "--use-angle=vulkan",
  "--disable-vulkan-surface"]), swiftshader: Object.freeze(["--enable-unsafe-webgpu", "--use-angle=swiftshader",
  "--enable-unsafe-swiftshader"]) })` selected by `GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU`; `GRAPHTY_EGL_LIB_DIR`, when set, is
  prepended to the Chromium child's `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` through `launch.env`; the browser project forwards
  `GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE`, `GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU`, `GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE` through `define` and `test.env`; the
  coverage thresholds 80 / 80 / 75 / 80 apply iff the selected project set is exactly `node` and `COVERAGE_DIR`
  is unset (the reporter list on the command line does not change that: `selectedProjects()` reads only
  `--project`).
- `package.json` scripts (contract 2.1): `build:all` = `npm run build && npm run build:bundle`; `lint` =
  `eslint && tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json && tsc -p tsconfig.strict-consumer.json`; `test:browser:ci` = `node
  scripts/run-browser-project.js`; `coverage` = `vitest run --project=node --coverage`. Workspace root
  (`/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/packages/package.json`): `build` = `pnpm -r run build:all`,
  `lint` = `pnpm -r run lint`, knip through `pnpm exec knip`.
- `test/setup/global.ts` (contract 5.1): `export function teardown(): void;` "Reads every worker's key log and
  asserts test/helpers/override-matrix.ts covers each key (throws with the uncovered keys, which fails the
  run)" -- so a green `--project=node` run IS the proof of the override-matrix coverage item.
- `test/helpers/noise-floor.ts` (contract 5.2): `export function adapterClass(caps: GpuCaps): string;` "The
  adapter class string of the running adapter: <vendor>-<architecture>-<runtime>"; the fixture file
  `test/fixtures/noise/<kernel>-<fixture>-<adapterClass>.json` holds `{ kernel, fixture, adapterClass, dtype,
  values }` (the 2.5 `writeNoiseFixture` command writes the same shape); `benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json`
  is the 5.6 document `{ recordedAt, adapters, rows: [{ id, kernel, fixture, comparison, a, b, maxRelError,
  maxAbsError, samples }], tolerances: { <id>: { value, basis, factor } } }` with `comparison` in `"twin" |
  "cross-adapter" | "oracle-f64"`, and "a `tolerances` entry is valid only when `value <= 10 x
  rows[basis].maxRelError`".
- The vitest 3.2.7 JSON reporter (verified in
  `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/packages/node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.VByaPkjc.js`
  lines 1698-1762): `{ numTotalTests, numPassedTests, numFailedTests, numPendingTests, success, startTime,
  testResults: [{ name: <absolute file path>, status: "passed" | "failed", startTime, endTime, assertionResults:
  [{ ancestorTitles: string[], fullName, title, status, duration, failureMessages }] }] }`; `--reporter=default
  --reporter=json --outputFile=<path>` writes it to `<path>` (the default reporter keeps printing to the
  terminal). Its `fullName` joins `ancestorTitles` and `title` with a single space
  (`packages/node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/index.VByaPkjc.js` line 1727 in 3.2.7); this task does not use
  it and joins `ancestorTitles` with " > " itself, then appends `title`.
- The GitHub REST API through `gh api` (gh 2.4.0 on the dev box, `{owner}/{repo}` placeholders resolved from the
  origin remote of the current directory): `repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/workflows/<file>/runs?branch=<b>`,
  `repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/<id>/jobs` (each job carries `steps[]` with `started_at` / `completed_at`),
  `repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/<id>/artifacts`; `gh run download <id> -n <artifact> -D <dir>`; `gh run
  watch <id>`.
- The environment (contract 8; spec 12.2): default-lane env `GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER=llvmpipe

  headless Chromium reaches the RTX 4070 SUPER only with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` (or `GRAPHTY_EGL_LIB_DIR`) pointing at
  `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/tmp/egl/root/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` plus the four `nvidia`

Produces:

- `packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms/docs/decisions/G2.md` -- the gate record P3 starts from; P3-T7 models
  `docs/decisions/G3.md` on its layout (sections 0-8 below) and reads its section 2 "observed slowdown factor"
  for the P3 lavapipe budget ("lavapipe runs the whole FA2 suite at gpuScale sizes in <= 3 min", spec 13 row
  P3).
- `packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms/CLAUDE.md` "Verified Platform Facts" -> "Settled at G2" rows (Step 10).
- `tmp/commit-p2.sh` with three modes: `check` (sanity checks, stages nothing), `code` (commit 1: every P2-T1 and
  P2-T2 file), `record` (commit 2: `G2.md` + `CLAUDE.md`), each accepting `--dry-run`.
- PLAN DECISION: the P2 code lands as ONE commit for the union of P2-T1 and P2-T2 (reason: the two were validated
  together by one green run in one working tree, as the section 7 preamble prescribes, and no separate
  build / lint / test of either half alone exists, so two commits could leave an unbuildable intermediate
  state); the G2 record is a SECOND commit because it cites the GitHub runs of the first (lane durations, the
  artifact), which cannot exist before the first is pushed.
- PLAN DECISION: the describe / it names in `G2.md` are `ancestorTitles` joined with " > " followed by `title`,
  both read from the vitest JSON reporter's `assertionResults` for the test files contract 5.5 assigns to each
  G2 item (the reporter's own `fullName` field joins with a space and is not used), extracted by
  `tmp/g2/g2-map.mjs` from the runs of this task (reason: the contract fixes files and assertions, not test titles; reading the titles from the run keeps the
  record true to what ran, and a file that did not run, or a test that skipped, is flagged instead of listed).

**Depends on / parallel with:** depends on P2-T1 and P2-T2 (both landed in the working tree and green
together); parallel with nothing -- the last task of P2. Owner actions inside this task: running
`tmp/commit-p2.sh code` and pushing (Step 11), deciding a budget miss (Step 12), running `tmp/commit-p2.sh
record` and pushing (Step 14).

All commands run from `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms` unless a
`cd` says otherwise; every path in the scripts is absolute, so they run from anywhere.

---

- [ ] **Step 1: Draft `tmp/commit-p2.sh` (the owner's commit script; its `check` mode is this task's precondition test) (8 min)**

Write `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/tmp/commit-p2.sh` with exactly this content (modelled on
`/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/tmp/commit-initial.sh`: `set -euo pipefail`, a `--dry-run`
flag, sanity checks, `GPG_TTY`, one explicit `git add` + heredoc message per logical commit through `git commit
-F -`, hooks narrowed with `-c core.hooksPath`, a "Next:" epilogue; no trailers of any kind):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# The P2 commits of webgpu-graph-algorithms (contract 7.3 rows P2-T1, P2-T2,
# P2-T3; spec 13 row P2). Drafted by P2-T3, run by the owner:
#
#   bash tmp/commit-p2.sh check              # sanity checks only; stages nothing
#   bash tmp/commit-p2.sh code               # commit 1: the P2-T1 + P2-T2 files
#   bash tmp/commit-p2.sh record             # commit 2: docs/decisions/G2.md + CLAUDE.md
#   bash tmp/commit-p2.sh code --dry-run     # stage and show what would happen (any mode)
#
# Two commits from two invocations because the G2 record cites the GitHub
# runs OF commit 1 (lane durations against T-12, the gpu-report.json artifact
# of the GPU lane), which cannot exist before commit 1 is pushed:
#   1. bash tmp/commit-p2.sh code && git push origin master
#   2. wait for CI and GPU on master (gh run watch <id>); P2-T3 fills
#      docs/decisions/G2.md from the runs (its steps 11-12)
#   3. bash tmp/commit-p2.sh record && git push origin master
#
# The only prompt you should see is your commit signing passphrase, once per
# commit. The repository has no hooks of its own (P0 deleted .husky/), so the
# commits run with an empty hooks directory and HUSKY=0; nothing is skipped
# that would otherwise have run.

set -euo pipefail

REPO="/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms"
PKG="packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms"
HOOKS="$REPO/tmp/githooks"
MODE="${1:-}"
DRY_RUN=0
[ "${2:-}" = "--dry-run" ] && DRY_RUN=1
case "$MODE" in
  check|code|record) ;;
  *) echo "usage: bash tmp/commit-p2.sh check|code|record [--dry-run]"; exit 2 ;;
esac

cd "$REPO"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The P2 files, from the contract's ownership map (7.3 rows P2-T1 and P2-T2).
# Lead d: within a phase no two tasks name the same file, so the lists are
# disjoint. NEW = created at P2 (untracked until this commit); EDIT = a P0 /
# P1 file P2 changed.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
P2_T1_NEW=(
  "$PKG/src/memory/lease.ts"
  "$PKG/src/kernel/batch.ts"
  "$PKG/src/kernel/uniform-ring.ts"
  "$PKG/test/memory/lease.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/batch.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/uniform-ring.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/profiler.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/state-roundtrip.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/uniform-layout.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/browser/batch.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/browser/lost.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/browser/state-roundtrip.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/browser/uniform-layout.test.ts"
)
P2_T1_EDIT=(
  "$PKG/src/memory/buffer-pool.ts"
  "$PKG/src/kernel/profiler.ts"
  "$PKG/src/context.ts"
  "$PKG/src/memory/residency.ts"
  "$PKG/test/device/lost.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/memory/residency.test.ts"
)
P2_T2_NEW=(
  "$PKG/src/wgsl/segmented-reduce.wgsl.ts"
  "$PKG/src/primitives/segmented-reduce.ts"
  "$PKG/test/oracle/segmented-reduce.ts"
  "$PKG/test/helpers/override-matrix.ts"
  "$PKG/test/primitives/segmented-reduce.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/sabotage/segmented-reduce.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/wgsl-compile.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/bind-group-budget.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/kernel/determinism.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/browser/compile-matrix.test.ts"
  "$PKG/test/limits/README.md"
)
P2_T2_EDIT=(
  "$PKG/src/kernels.ts"
  "$PKG/test/helpers/caps-tables.ts"
  "$PKG/test/helpers/sabotage.ts"
  "$PKG/test/setup/global.ts"
  "$PKG/benchmarks/results/noise-floor.json"
)
# P2-T3: the record (commit 2).
RECORD=(
  "$PKG/docs/decisions/G2.md"
  "$PKG/CLAUDE.md"
)
# The per-adapter noise fixtures of segmented-reduce (P2-T2): one file per
# adapter class, at least the three adapters of spec 11.5.
FIXTURES=()
while IFS= read -r f; do
  [ -n "$f" ] && FIXTURES+=("$f")
done < <(compgen -G "$PKG/test/fixtures/noise/segmented-reduce-*.json" || true)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# An empty hooks directory: nothing else exists to run.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
setup_hooks() { mkdir -p "$HOOKS"; }
cleanup_hooks() { rm -rf "$HOOKS"; }
trap cleanup_hooks EXIT
export HUSKY=0

# Let gpg-agent find the terminal so pinentry can prompt here.
if tty -s; then export GPG_TTY="$(tty)"; fi

commit_with() {
  # commit_with  -- message arrives on stdin
  if [ "$DRY_RUN" = "1" ]; then
    echo "    dry run: not committing"
    return 0
  fi
  setup_hooks
  echo "    committing. You will be asked for your signing passphrase."
  git -c core.hooksPath="$HOOKS" commit -F -
  cleanup_hooks
  echo "    $(git log -1 --format='%h %G? %s')"
}

staged_is_empty() { git diff --cached --quiet; }

ERRORS=0
note_error() { echo "ERROR: $*"; ERRORS=$((ERRORS + 1)); }

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sanity checks shared by every mode.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
echo "==> Checking the working tree ($MODE)"
git rev-parse --verify --quiet HEAD >/dev/null || { echo "ERROR: no HEAD; P0 and P1 must be committed first"; exit 1; }
if [ "$(git symbolic-ref --short HEAD)" != "master" ]; then
  echo "ERROR: expected branch master, got $(git symbolic-ref --short HEAD)"
  exit 1
fi
if ! git diff --cached --quiet; then
  echo "ERROR: something is already staged. This script stages each commit"
  echo "itself and would sweep it in. Unstage first: git reset"
  exit 1
fi
# The P0 root .gitignore (contract 1.1) lists node_modules, dist, coverage,
# tmp/, .tmp/, *.tsbuildinfo, benchmarks/out/, browser-results.json,
# gpu-report.json, *.log, .DS_Store -- no .env (P0 deleted .env and
# .env.example). tmp/ holds research clones with their own .git directories;
# the run outputs of Steps 2-8 (coverage/, browser-results.json,
# gpu-report.json under the package) must not be swept in either. The
# benchmarks/out/ pattern carries a middle slash, so it is anchored to the
# .gitignore that holds it and cannot be checked by path here; an untracked
# benchmarks/out/ shows up in the UNEXPECTED list below instead.
for p in tmp dist node_modules "$PKG/coverage" "$PKG/browser-results.json" "$PKG/gpu-report.json"; do
  git check-ignore -q "$p" || { echo "ERROR: $p is not ignored"; exit 1; }
done
if [ -n "$(git ls-files --others --exclude-standard | grep -E '(^|/)\.git/' | head -1)" ]; then
  echo "ERROR: an untracked path contains a .git directory"; exit 1
fi

# Every dirty path must be in one of the lists: an explicit `git add` would
# silently leave anything else behind.
declare -A ALLOWED=()
for f in "${P2_T1_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T1_EDIT[@]}" "${P2_T2_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T2_EDIT[@]}" "${RECORD[@]}" "${FIXTURES[@]}"; do
  ALLOWED["$f"]=1
done
UNEXPECTED=()
while IFS= read -r line; do
  [ -n "$line" ] || continue
  path="${line:3}"
  [ -n "${ALLOWED[$path]:-}" ] || UNEXPECTED+=("$line")
done < <(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Mode-specific checks.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
CODE_DONE=0
if [ "$MODE" = "check" ] || [ "$MODE" = "code" ]; then
  CODE_FILES=("${P2_T1_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T1_EDIT[@]}" "${P2_T2_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T2_EDIT[@]}" "${FIXTURES[@]}")
  CLEAN=0
  for f in "${CODE_FILES[@]}"; do
    if git ls-files --error-unmatch "$f" >/dev/null 2>&1 && [ -z "$(git status --porcelain -- "$f")" ]; then
      CLEAN=$((CLEAN + 1))
    fi
  done
  if [ "$CLEAN" -eq "${#CODE_FILES[@]}" ] && [ "${#FIXTURES[@]}" -ge 3 ]; then
    # Every P2 code file is tracked and clean: 'code' already ran.
    CODE_DONE=1
    echo "    every P2 code file is committed already (HEAD $(git log -1 --format=%h)); 'code' has nothing to do"
  else
    for f in "${P2_T1_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T2_NEW[@]}"; do
      [ -e "$f" ] || note_error "expected $f to exist (created at P2)"
      if git ls-files --error-unmatch "$f" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo "WARNING: $f is already tracked (the contract lists it as created at P2)"
      fi
    done
    for f in "${P2_T1_EDIT[@]}" "${P2_T2_EDIT[@]}"; do
      [ -e "$f" ] || note_error "expected $f to exist (edited at P2)"
      git ls-files --error-unmatch "$f" >/dev/null 2>&1 || note_error "$f is not tracked (expected an edit of a P0 / P1 file)"
      if git ls-files --error-unmatch "$f" >/dev/null 2>&1 && git diff --quiet -- "$f"; then
        echo "WARNING: $f shows no change (the contract lists it as edited at P2)"
      fi
    done
    if [ "${#FIXTURES[@]}" -lt 3 ]; then
      note_error "expected >= 3 files $PKG/test/fixtures/noise/segmented-reduce-*.json (one per adapter class), found ${#FIXTURES[@]}"
    fi
    echo "    P2 code files: $(( ${#P2_T1_NEW[@]} + ${#P2_T2_NEW[@]} )) new, $(( ${#P2_T1_EDIT[@]} + ${#P2_T2_EDIT[@]} )) edited, ${#FIXTURES[@]} noise fixtures"
  fi
  for f in "${RECORD[@]}"; do
    if [ -e "$f" ] && [ -n "$(git status --porcelain -- "$f")" ]; then
      echo "    record file present (left for 'record'): $f"
    elif [ -e "$f" ]; then
      echo "    record file present and unchanged: $f"
    else
      echo "    record file absent (P2-T3 writes it): $f"
    fi
  done
  if [ "${#UNEXPECTED[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
    if [ "$MODE" = "check" ]; then echo "WARNING: changes outside the P2 lists (resolve before 'code'):"; else note_error "changes outside the P2 lists; stash them or add them to the lists:"; fi
    printf '      %s\n' "${UNEXPECTED[@]}"
  fi
fi

if [ "$MODE" = "record" ]; then
  for f in "${RECORD[@]}"; do [ -e "$f" ] || note_error "expected $f to exist"; done
  # Template fields are {{name}} with lowercase letters and hyphens only, so a
  # GitHub Actions expression such as ${{ github.run_id }} (space after the
  # braces) quoted in CLAUDE.md's Testing section never matches.
  FIELD='\{\{[a-z-]+\}\}'
  if [ -e "$PKG/docs/decisions/G2.md" ] && grep -Eq "$FIELD" "$PKG/docs/decisions/G2.md"; then
    note_error "docs/decisions/G2.md still has unfilled fields: $(grep -Eo "$FIELD" "$PKG/docs/decisions/G2.md" | sort -u | tr '\n' ' ')"
  fi
  if [ -e "$PKG/CLAUDE.md" ] && grep -Eq "$FIELD" "$PKG/CLAUDE.md"; then
    note_error "CLAUDE.md still has unfilled fields: $(grep -Eo "$FIELD" "$PKG/CLAUDE.md" | sort -u | tr '\n' ' ')"
  fi
  grep -q 'Settled at G2' "$PKG/CLAUDE.md" 2>/dev/null || note_error "CLAUDE.md lacks the 'Settled at G2' block"
  if grep -P -q '[^\x00-\x7F]' "${RECORD[@]}" 2>/dev/null; then note_error "non-ASCII bytes in the record files"; fi
  for f in "${P2_T1_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T1_EDIT[@]}" "${P2_T2_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T2_EDIT[@]}" "${FIXTURES[@]}"; do
    if [ -n "$(git status --porcelain -- "$f")" ]; then note_error "$f is still uncommitted; run 'code' first"; fi
  done
  if [ "${#UNEXPECTED[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
    note_error "changes outside the record files:"
    printf '      %s\n' "${UNEXPECTED[@]}"
  fi
fi

if [ "$ERRORS" -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "$ERRORS error(s) above"
  exit 1
fi
if [ "$MODE" = "check" ]; then
  if [ "$CODE_DONE" = "1" ]; then
    echo "    master, clean index, tmp/ and the run outputs ignored; ready for: record"
  else
    echo "    master, clean index, tmp/ and the run outputs ignored; ready for: code"
  fi
  exit 0
fi
if [ "$MODE" = "code" ] && [ "$CODE_DONE" = "1" ]; then
  echo "    nothing to commit; next: bash tmp/commit-p2.sh record"
  exit 0
fi

# ===========================================================================
# COMMIT 1 -- the P2 code (P2-T1 + P2-T2)
# ===========================================================================
if [ "$MODE" = "code" ]; then
  echo
  echo "==> P2 code: batch + dispatch infrastructure (P2-T1, P2-T2)"
  git add -- "${P2_T1_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T1_EDIT[@]}" "${P2_T2_NEW[@]}" "${P2_T2_EDIT[@]}" "${FIXTURES[@]}"
  if staged_is_empty; then
    echo "    nothing staged; skipping"
  else
    git diff --cached --stat | tail -1
    echo "    left uncommitted for 'record': ${RECORD[*]}"
    commit_with <<'MSGEOF'
feat(webgpu-graph-algorithms): batch and dispatch infrastructure (P2)

The kernel layer of the plan's section 5 is complete and tested, and the
primitives P3 needs exist (spec 13 row P2; contract 7.3 rows P2-T1 and
P2-T2):

  memory/lease            Lease: the scope object that returns every scratch
                          buffer to the pool when an algorithm resolves or
                          rejects; BufferPool.lease()
  kernel/batch            CommandBatch: N dispatches and copies recorded into
                          one encoder and submitted once, one borrowed
                          staging slot for the batch's readback, and the
                          pending-error check right after submit() so a bad
                          bind group rejects ITS OWN readback under Dawn-node
                          (E_VALIDATION with the batch id)
  kernel/uniform-ring     UniformRing: 256-byte-stride params slots read
                          through dynamic offsets inside a batch
  kernel/profiler         Profiler: timestampWrites per pass when the feature
                          was granted, the quantised flag, no-ops otherwise
  context / residency     BatchHost members, profiler creation, onLost
                          fan-out, pipeline warm-up; residentBytes with its
                          once-only warning and clearOnLoss
  primitives/segmented-reduce
                          segmentedReduce in its thread-per-row tier over the
                          row-permutation dummy pattern, the Gunrock-style
                          value snippet and the subgroup twin; degree tiers
                          answer E_UNSUPPORTED until P4
  wgsl/segmented-reduce   the normative body of contract 4.5

Tests (contract 5.5, P2): lease; batch; uniform ring; profiler; the Fa2
state round trip on both runtimes; device loss mid-batch on both runtimes
with recovery from a fresh adapter; the misaligned-uniform negative test
(rejected by Chromium, accepted by Dawn-node); segmented-reduce against
its f64 oracle including all-equal weights and a hub row, both twins
in-process and bitwise across two runs; its three sabotage rows (each
failing by >= 10x) with SABOTAGE_PHASES gaining "P2"; its noise fixtures
from the three adapters; the bounded override matrix compiled on the real
device, on Dawn backend=null and in Chromium; the bind-group budget;
determinism; and the pipeline-key teardown that fails the node run when a
key escapes the matrix. CAPS_INTEL_XE (subgroups 8/32) joins the caps
tables. test/limits/ holds a README until P4.
MSGEOF
  fi
fi

# ===========================================================================
# COMMIT 2 -- the G2 record (P2-T3)
# ===========================================================================
if [ "$MODE" = "record" ]; then
  echo
  echo "==> G2 record: docs/decisions/G2.md and the CLAUDE.md facts (P2-T3)"
  git add -- "${RECORD[@]}"
  if staged_is_empty; then
    echo "    nothing staged; skipping"
  else
    git diff --cached --stat | tail -1
    commit_with <<'MSGEOF'
docs(webgpu-graph-algorithms): record the G2 gate

docs/decisions/G2.md maps every item of the G2 checklist (contract 7.3)
to the test that proved it on lavapipe, on the RTX 4070 SUPER, on
SwiftShader and on NVIDIA Chromium, records the lane durations of the P2
code commit's GitHub runs against T-12 (default lane <= 15 min, GPU lane
<= 20 min), the gpu-report.json artifact of the GPU lane, the
segmented-reduce noise-floor rows from the three adapters, and the
owner decisions the phase needed (none unless section 6 says so).

CLAUDE.md "Verified Platform Facts" gains the two facts P2 settled: the
uncapturederror delivery order (synchronous under Dawn-node, asynchronous
in Chromium) and the misaligned-uniform outcome per runtime (accepted by
Dawn-node, rejected by Chromium 139), each with the test that settled it.
MSGEOF
  fi
fi

# ===========================================================================
echo
if [ "$DRY_RUN" = "1" ]; then
  echo "Dry run. Nothing committed; the group above stays staged."
  echo "Unstage with: git reset"
  exit 0
fi

echo "==> Done."
git log --oneline -3
echo
if [ -n "$(git status --short | head -1)" ]; then
  echo "Still uncommitted:"
  git status --short
  echo
fi
if [ "$MODE" = "code" ]; then
  echo "Next:"

  echo "and when docs/decisions/G2.md has no {{field}} left:  bash tmp/commit-p2.sh record"
else
  echo "Next:"

  echo "G2 is closed; P3 may start (spec 13 rule a: the gate is green on both lanes)."
fi
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs` and read the P1 run's fixed overhead (6 min)**

Write `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs`:

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
// tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs -- job, step and artifact facts of the latest
// COMPLETED run of a workflow on a branch (optionally at a given commit),
// read from the GitHub REST API through `gh api`, plus the T-12 verdict for
// the lane's test job (spec 10.4: default lane <= 15 min, GPU lane <= 20 min).
//
//   node tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs ci.yml master            # latest completed push run on master
//   node tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs gpu.yml master <sha>     # the GPU run of that commit
//
// Without a sha the latest completed PUSH run is taken: a nightly
// `schedule` run of gpu.yml whose `changed` job found master unmoved skips
// test-gpu (contract 2.9, spec 12.6), concludes "success" with a skipped
// Test job and would prove nothing about the budget. With a sha every event
// is accepted (the run of that commit is the one wanted).
//
// Exit 0 when the run concluded "success" and its test job ran green within
// budget; 1 otherwise (no completed run yet, a failed run, a skipped test
// job, over budget); 2 on usage.
// Prints markdown list items: the run line, one line per job with its T-12
// verdict, the step lines (indented, minutes) and the artifact lines. G2.md
// sections 1 and 2 paste this output verbatim.
import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process";

const REPO_DIR = "/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms";
const BUDGET_MIN = { "ci.yml": 15, "gpu.yml": 20 };

/**
 * One GET against the GitHub REST API through gh; the {owner}/{repo}
 * placeholders resolve from the origin remote of REPO_DIR.
 * @param {string} path - the API path
 * @returns {any} the parsed JSON body
 */
function gh(path) {
    return JSON.parse(execFileSync("gh", ["api", path], { cwd: REPO_DIR, encoding: "utf8", maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 }));
}

/**
 * Minutes between two ISO-8601 timestamps, two decimals.
 * @param {string} from - start
 * @param {string} to - end
 * @returns {string} the minutes as text
 */
function minutes(from, to) {
    return ((Date.parse(to) - Date.parse(from)) / 60000).toFixed(2);
}

const args = process.argv.slice(2);
const workflow = args[0];
const branch = args[1] === undefined ? "master" : args[1];
const sha = args[2];
if (workflow !== "ci.yml" && workflow !== "gpu.yml") {
    console.error("usage: node tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs <ci.yml|gpu.yml> [branch] [sha]");
    process.exit(2);
}

const runs = gh(`repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/workflows/${workflow}/runs?branch=${branch}&per_page=30`).workflow_runs;
const run = runs.find(
    (r) => r.status === "completed" && (sha === undefined ? r.event === "push" : r.head_sha.startsWith(sha)),
);
if (run === undefined) {
    const at = sha === undefined ? " (event push)" : ` at ${sha}`;
    console.error(`no completed run of ${workflow} on ${branch}${at} yet (${runs.length} runs listed; block on it with: gh run watch <id>)`);
    process.exit(1);
}
console.log(`- ${workflow} run ${run.id} (#${run.run_number}, event ${run.event}, commit ${run.head_sha.slice(0, 12)}, conclusion ${run.conclusion}): ${run.html_url}`);

const jobs = gh(`repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/${run.id}/jobs?per_page=100`).jobs;
let withinBudget = true;
for (const job of jobs) {
    const runner = typeof job.runner_name === "string" && job.runner_name !== "" ? ` on ${job.runner_name}` : "";
    const labels = Array.isArray(job.labels) && job.labels.length > 0 ? ` [${job.labels.join(", ")}]` : "";
    const timed = typeof job.started_at === "string" && typeof job.completed_at === "string";
    const jobMin = timed ? minutes(job.started_at, job.completed_at) : "n/a";
    console.log(`- job "${job.name}": ${job.conclusion}, ${jobMin} min${runner}${labels}`);
    if (job.name.startsWith("Test")) {
        const budget = BUDGET_MIN[workflow];
        // A skipped or failed test job never passes T-12, whatever its stamps say.
        const ok = timed && job.conclusion === "success" && Number(jobMin) <= budget;
        withinBudget = withinBudget && ok;
        console.log(`    - T-12: ${jobMin} min ${ok ? "<=" : ">"} ${budget} min budget (job ${job.conclusion}) -> ${ok ? "PASS" : "FAIL"}`);
    }
    const steps = Array.isArray(job.steps) ? job.steps : [];
    for (const step of steps) {
        if (typeof step.started_at === "string" && typeof step.completed_at === "string") {
            console.log(`    - ${minutes(step.started_at, step.completed_at).padStart(6)} min  ${String(step.conclusion).padEnd(9)}  ${step.name}`);
        }
    }
}

const artifacts = gh(`repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/${run.id}/artifacts`).artifacts;
for (const a of artifacts) {
    console.log(`- artifact "${a.name}": ${a.size_in_bytes} bytes, expires ${a.expires_at}`);
}
if (artifacts.length === 0) {
    console.log("- artifacts: none");
}
process.exit(run.conclusion === "success" && withinBudget ? 0 : 1);
```

actions/checkout@v4`, `pnpm/action-setup`, `setup-node`, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `Install Mesa
lavapipe`, `pnpm run build`, `pnpm -r run lint`, `pnpm exec knip`, `Node suite on lavapipe (coverage with thresholds; subgroup twins in-process, plus the no-subgroups pass)`,
`Cache Playwright browsers`, the install step that ran, `Browser smoke on SwiftShader`, `Run
actions/upload-artifact@v4`, the `Post` cleanup steps), and `artifact "coverage-webgpu-graph-algorithms"`; the second names a `gpu.yml` run with the
`changed` job, `job "Test (NVIDIA T4, GitHub-hosted)"` with its T-12 line (`(job success) -> PASS`) and the
artifact `gpu-results-<run id>`. Exit code 0 for both (`echo $?`). Without a sha the script skips the nightly
`schedule` runs of gpu.yml (their `changed` job skips `test-gpu` when master has not moved, contract 2.9), so
the run named is the P1-T7 push run, not last night's skipped one.

Compute the default lane's fixed overhead in minutes = the `Test (software adapters)` job minutes MINUS the
minutes of the two steps `Node suite on lavapipe (coverage with thresholds; subgroup twins in-process, plus the
no-subgroups pass)` and `Browser smoke on SwiftShader`; round up to a whole

(checkout, pnpm install from cache, apt, build, lint, knip, the Playwright cache). If `gh` reports no completed

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `tmp/g2/time-default-lane.sh` and time the default lane locally on lavapipe (15 min)**

Write `/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms/tmp/g2/time-default-lane.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# tmp/g2/time-default-lane.sh -- times the default-lane commands of
# .github/workflows/ci.yml (contract 2.9) locally on lavapipe, in the order
# the workflow runs them and with the workflow's environment (spec 12.2), and
# keeps the JSON reporter output of every vitest pass for tmp/g2/g2-map.mjs.
#
#   bash tmp/g2/time-default-lane.sh [overhead-minutes]
#
# overhead-minutes: the GitHub job's time OUTSIDE the two test steps
# (checkout, pnpm install, apt, build, lint, knip, the Playwright cache),
# read from the P1 run with `node tmp/g2/lane-durations.mjs ci.yml master`
# before the P2 push (default 5, spec 12.6). The local pre-check flags the
# suite when 3 x (local test steps) + overhead > 15 min (T-12); the factor 3
# is the allowance for ubuntu-latest's 4 vCPUs against this box. The gate
# itself is the GitHub measurement (G2.md section 2), never this number.
#
# Not timed here: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` (cached locally),
# `apt-get install mesa-vulkan-drivers` (lavapipe is installed) and the
# Playwright install (chromium-1181 is present). Every step runs even when an
# earlier one is red, so the timings are complete; the exit status is 1 when
# any step was red.
set -euo pipefail

REPO=/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms
PKG=$REPO/packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms
OUT=$REPO/tmp/g2
OVERHEAD_MIN=${1:-5}
LOG=$OUT/default-lane-local.txt
mkdir -p "$OUT"
: > "$LOG"

# The default-lane environment of the Node steps (spec 12.2 / contract 2.9).
export GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER=llvmpipe
export GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any
export VK_DRIVER_FILES=/usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.x86_64.json
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp
unset GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS GRAPHTY_EGL_LIB_DIR GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE GRAPHTY_GPU_INSPECT

STATUS=0
LAST_SECONDS=0
TEST_SECONDS=0

# timed <label> <dir> <log file> <command> [args]: runs the command in <dir>,
# tees its output to <log file>, appends "<label> <seconds> s exit <rc>" to
# $LOG and keeps going on failure.
timed() {
    local label=$1 dir=$2 log=$3
    shift 3
    local start end rc
    start=$(date +%s)
    set +e
    ( cd "$dir" && "$@" 2>&1 | tee "$log" )
    rc=$?
    set -e
    end=$(date +%s)
    LAST_SECONDS=$((end - start))
    printf '%-36s %6d s  exit %d\n' "$label" "$LAST_SECONDS" "$rc" | tee -a "$LOG"
    if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ]; then STATUS=1; fi
}

{
    echo "default lane mirrored locally on lavapipe"
    echo "date: $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)  cpus: $(nproc)  node: $(node --version)  pnpm: $(pnpm --version)"
    echo "env: GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER=llvmpipe GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any VK_DRIVER_FILES=$VK_DRIVER_FILES XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp"
} | tee -a "$LOG"

timed "build (pnpm run build)" "$REPO/packages" "$OUT/build.log" pnpm run build
timed "lint (pnpm -r run lint)" "$REPO/packages" "$OUT/lint.log" pnpm -r run lint
timed "knip (pnpm exec knip)" "$REPO/packages" "$OUT/knip.log" pnpm exec knip
timed "node --coverage (lavapipe)" "$PKG" "$OUT/node-lavapipe.log" \
    pnpm exec vitest run --project=node --coverage --reporter=default --reporter=json --outputFile="$OUT/node-lavapipe.json"
TEST_SECONDS=$((TEST_SECONDS + LAST_SECONDS))
timed "node no-subgroups pass (lavapipe)" "$PKG" "$OUT/node-nosubgroups-lavapipe.log" \
    env GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS=1 pnpm exec vitest run --project=node test/primitives test/layouts --passWithNoTests --reporter=default --reporter=json --outputFile="$OUT/node-nosubgroups-lavapipe.json"
TEST_SECONDS=$((TEST_SECONDS + LAST_SECONDS))
timed "browser smoke (SwiftShader)" "$PKG" "$OUT/browser-swiftshader.log" \
    env -u GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER -u VK_DRIVER_FILES GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=swiftshader GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=any node scripts/run-browser-project.js
TEST_SECONDS=$((TEST_SECONDS + LAST_SECONDS))
cp "$PKG/browser-results.json" "$OUT/browser-swiftshader.json"

TEST_MIN=$(awk -v t="$TEST_SECONDS" 'BEGIN { printf "%.2f", t / 60 }')
PREDICTED=$(awk -v t="$TEST_SECONDS" -v o="$OVERHEAD_MIN" 'BEGIN { printf "%.2f", 3 * t / 60 + o }')
VERDICT=$(awk -v t="$TEST_SECONDS" -v o="$OVERHEAD_MIN" 'BEGIN { if (3 * t / 60 + o <= 15) print "PASS"; else print "FLAG" }')
{
    echo "test steps total: $TEST_SECONDS s = $TEST_MIN min"
    echo "pre-check: 3 x $TEST_MIN min + $OVERHEAD_MIN min overhead = $PREDICTED min vs 15 min (T-12) -> $VERDICT"
    echo "steps exit status: $STATUS (0 = every step green)"
} | tee -a "$LOG"
exit $STATUS
```

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# tmp/g2/evidence-nvidia.sh -- the NVIDIA legs of the G2 evidence on the dev
# box (the GPU lane's vendor): the node project on the RTX 4070 SUPER and the
# browser project on NVIDIA Chromium, both with the JSON reporter so that
# tmp/g2/g2-map.mjs can read the test names and statuses.
#
#   bash tmp/g2/evidence-nvidia.sh
#
# Node: GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER unset (Dawn picks the discrete GPU),
# GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware (a software adapter is a failure, never a
# skip). Browser: GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=nvidia (the four Vulkan flags of
# vitest.config.ts BROWSER_FLAGS) with libEGL.so.1 reachable through both
# GRAPHTY_EGL_LIB_DIR (vitest.config.ts prepends it to the Chromium child's
# LD_LIBRARY_PATH) and LD_LIBRARY_PATH itself (HEADLESS_GPU_REPORT.md).
set -euo pipefail

REPO=/home/apowers/Projects/webgpu-graph-algorithms
PKG=$REPO/packages/webgpu-graph-algorithms
OUT=$REPO/tmp/g2
EGL=$REPO/tmp/egl/root/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu
mkdir -p "$OUT"
export XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/tmp
unset GRAPHTY_GPU_ADAPTER VK_DRIVER_FILES GRAPHTY_GPU_NO_SUBGROUPS GRAPHTY_NOISE_FLOOR_WRITE GRAPHTY_GPU_INSPECT
[ -e "$EGL/libEGL.so.1" ] || { echo "ERROR: $EGL/libEGL.so.1 is missing (HEADLESS_GPU_REPORT.md appendix D)"; exit 1; }

STATUS=0
cd "$PKG"

echo "==> node project on NVIDIA (GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware)"
if ! GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware pnpm exec vitest run --project=node --reporter=default --reporter=json --outputFile="$OUT/node-nvidia.json" 2>&1 | tee "$OUT/node-nvidia.log"; then
    STATUS=1
fi

echo "==> browser project on NVIDIA Chromium (GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=nvidia, libEGL on LD_LIBRARY_PATH)"
if ! GRAPHTY_BROWSER_GPU=nvidia GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware GRAPHTY_EGL_LIB_DIR="$EGL" LD_LIBRARY_PATH="$EGL${LD_LIBRARY_PATH:+:$LD_LIBRARY_PATH}" node scripts/run-browser-project.js 2>&1 | tee "$OUT/browser-nvidia.log"; then
    STATUS=1
fi
cp browser-results.json "$OUT/browser-nvidia.json"

echo "==> adapters seen"
grep -m1 -io 'NVIDIA GeForce[^"]*' "$OUT/node-nvidia.log" || echo "node log: no NVIDIA description line"
grep -m1 -io 'vendor[^,]*nvidia[^,]*' "$OUT/browser-nvidia.log" || echo "browser log: no nvidia vendor line (check GRAPHTY_GPU_REQUIRE=hardware did not fail the run)"
echo "status: $STATUS"
exit $STATUS
```
